| 2026-10-31 | M23 | D87 (implementer decision): the liquid-dsp halfband references are built from the pinned release tarball (the one of the DC blocker, sha256 in `benches/refs/build.sh`) by one wrapper, `benches/refs/liquid-halfband.c`, that is a translation unit of liquid in the sense of `filter_crcf.c`: it defines liquid's type macros and `#include`s the unpacked `resamp2.proto.c` and `firhilb.proto.c`, so the per-sample code is liquid's own and nothing is copied into the repository (the DC blocker's vendored copies are left as they are). The dot products (`dotprod_crcf.c`, `dotprod_rrrf.c`), window buffers and support files are compiled from the same tarball. liquid's `_create` functions design a Kaiser filter, which the references must not do (our taps are injected): the wrapper builds the objects from a given prototype with the code of `_create` that follows the design, and the four design functions that the unused `_create` and `_recreate` of the prototype files name are stubs that abort. The six functions that the DC blocker's vendored `liquid-dotprod.c` also defines (`dotprod_{crcf,rrrf}_{create,destroy,execute}`) are renamed with `-D` in the halfband objects, so that both libraries link into one binary (the first link failed on exactly these). One library per level, `libliquidhb-<level>.a`, holds both dot-product methods and the wrapper chooses per object (`LIQUID_RUNTIME_PORT` for the fallback entries, the level's method for the SIMD entries); both are compiled for the level with `-O3 -ffp-contract=off` | vendoring would copy 3000 lines of MIT code into the repository and hide the release it came from; including the release keeps the pin (sha256) and the licence in one place. A run-time choice per object is the public `dotprod_*_runtime_select` of liquid, and one library per level avoids the symbol clash that two builds of the same object files in one binary would cause. FMA contraction is off on both sides (D5), as for the CMSIS-DSP references |
| 2026-10-31 | M23 | D88 (implementer decision): the levels of liquid's dot products follow the maintainer decision of 2026-10-31 literally: `dotprod_crcf` SSE at `sse2` and `sse4.2`, AVX at `avx2`, AVX-512F at `avx512`, NEON at `neon`, the portable loop with `-msimd128` at `simd128`; `dotprod_rrrf` (`resamp2_rrrf` and the `firhilbf` of `real_to_complex`) the portable loop at `sse2`, SSE with `-DHAVE_SSE3=1` (the SSE3 horizontal add that liquid's autotools build enables where the compiler has SSE3) at `sse4.2`, then as `crcf`. `-DHAVE_SSE3=1` is passed to `dotprod_rrrf.c` only, and only on x86_64 | the decision names SSE3 `hadd` as the reason for referencing the real dot product from `sse4.2`; liquid's CMake build does not define `HAVE_SSE3` (then the loop adds the four lanes in scalar code and needs SSE only), its autotools build does, so the flag is what makes the `sse4.2` entry the loop the decision means |
| 2026-10-31 | M23 | D89 (implementer decision): the taps of the references. The workload taps are `halfband_taps` of 47 taps with a Kaiser window of 70 dB (centre tap exactly 0.5 after the scaling to sum 1, 24 non-zero taps at the odd offsets; as the 47 taps of libairspy). liquid's `resamp2` takes a prototype of `4 m + 1 = 49` taps whose outermost taps are zero: the 47 taps are placed between two zero taps and divided by the centre tap, because liquid's delay branch has the gain 1 where SDRk's has the centre tap; `resamp2` gets `set_scale(centre)` (its own scale, applied inside its sum), `firhilbf` has no scale, so its output is `j / centre` times SDRk's `real_to_complex` and the benchmark maps it back outside the counted region. libairspy's converters get the 47 taps; their output is `-conj` of SDRk's, and `iqconverter_int16` hard-wires the centre tap 0.5, which our design has exactly. All three mappings are verified against the definition in `f64` by `benches/cref-sys/tests/halfband_refs.rs` (the liquid and float libairspy outputs agree to 1e-5 per sample) | the maintainer asked for our taps in the references so that the accuracy is comparable; the mapping constants follow from the structure of the three libraries (liquid's real part is the delay branch and its imaginary part the filter where SDRk's is the opposite, libairspy shifts by the other sign of fs / 4) and were found with a numerical comparison and then written into the test |
| 2026-10-31 | M23 | D90 (implementer decision): libairspy v1.0.10 as a reference. `benches/refs/airspy-iqconv.c` includes the pinned release's `iqconverter_float.c` / `iqconverter_int16.c` and exports the conversion without `remove_dc` (libairspy's `process` is `remove_dc` and `translate_fs_4`; the static `translate_fs_4` is called directly). libairspy converts in place, so the wrapper copies the input first (a counted `memcpy` of 4096 values, under 0.3 % of the work). libairspy enables its SSE2 code for FreeBSD only (`USE_SSE2` is defined in the `__FreeBSD__` branch, whose `acc[0]` access needs GCC or Clang vector extensions), so the SSE2 build compiles the file with that branch (the macro is set after the system headers). For the 47-tap kernel the unrolled 24-tap FIR loop has no intrinsics, so the SSE2 build differs from the generic one in the rotation of `translate_fs_4` only. `iqconverter_int16_reset` of v1.0.10 clears half of the delay line and half of the FIR queue (it uses `sizeof(int16_t)` for 32-bit entries), so the first 12 outputs of a fresh converter hold whatever the allocator returned (21 dB SNR against the model on 4096 samples before the fix, 81.5 dB after): the wrapper clears both completely. wasi-libc has no `memalign`, which libairspy's GNU branch uses: a shim over `posix_memalign` on wasm32. Where libairspy is used: `real_to_complex` C32 at `sse2` as the second gate (the SSE2 build; first written as information, corrected by D101), `real_to_complex_fallback` as information (the generic build, compiled for the level), `real_to_complex_cq15` and `real_to_complex_cq15_fallback` as the comparison (`iqconverter_int16`, generic C compiled for the level), `info` until the maintainer decides (I23-1) | the measurement is of the conversion; the defects of the pinned release are worked around in the wrapper and recorded, never by patching the release; the `info` status of the CQ15 entries is decision 4 of 2026-10-31 (D70) |
| 2026-10-31 | M23 | D91 (implementer decision): the CMSIS-DSP composition for `halfband_decimate_cq15_fallback` and `halfband_decimate_real_q15_fallback` and as information next to the SIMD entries. CMSIS-DSP has no halfband decimator, so per channel: the polyphase split of the input into its even and odd samples (a counted loop in the measurement binary), `arm_fir_q15` with the 24 compacted taps on the even samples (the filter branch), `arm_scale_q15` with the centre tap on the odd samples written behind 12 samples of the previous call (the delay branch), `arm_add_q15` (added to the CMSIS-DSP sources of `build.sh`). The complex type runs it on the planar `re` and `im`, which are prepared once and not counted, as the FIR family does (`fir_family.rs`). The result is a lower accuracy class than ours (truncating `arm_fir_q15` and `arm_scale_q15`, a double rounding): 82.5 dB against the exact sum of ours (on the count input, SNR against the `f64` model), recorded with the numbers | the maintainer asked for exactly this composition; counting the split is part of the algorithm (CMSIS-DSP's FIR needs the phases apart), while the re/im planarisation is only a data layout that the other CMSIS-DSP entries of the table also leave out |
| 2026-10-31 | M23 | D92 (implementer decision): the statuses of `benches/limits.json` for the M23 entries, all six levels alike: `halfband_decimate`, `halfband_decimate_real`, `real_to_complex` `release` (liquid-dsp is the gate; the own-kernel comparisons are information, D69); `halfband_decimate_cq15`, `halfband_decimate_real_q15` `info` with the waiver text of the Q15 entries (D21, D32); `real_to_complex_cq15` `info`, provisional (D70, open item I23-1); the fallback entries `halfband_decimate_fallback`, `halfband_decimate_real_fallback`, `real_to_complex_fallback`, `halfband_decimate_cq15_fallback`, `halfband_decimate_real_q15_fallback` `release`; `real_to_complex_cq15_fallback` `info`, provisional (the decision names libairspy `iqconverter_int16` for the CQ15 `real_to_complex` without separating its fallback; the same reference gates both and both wait for I23-1); the four workload entries `halfband_ref_*` `info`. The fallback entry of `real_to_complex` CQ15 and `real_to_complex_cq15` report our fallback and the separate shift as information. No per-entry `limit` field was added to `limits.json`: with the own-kernel comparisons as information only, every judged comparison has an external reference and the global caps apply | the decision gave the references and the CQ15 `real_to_complex` status; everything it did not mention follows the table of D69; the per-entry limit would have been needed only to judge a ratio against our own kernel (<= 0.60 of `fir_decimate` x2), which D69 made information |
| 2026-10-31 | M23 | D93 (implementer decision): the own-kernel comparisons. `Reference::OwnKernel(kernel)` runs `ours` of another entry of the table and reports it in `informational` as `ours:<kernel>`; the entry names it through the new field `KernelSpec::own`. The workloads are the entries `halfband_ref_fir_decimate` / `_cq15` (our `fir_decimate` by 2 with the 47 taps, zeros included: what `halfband_decimate` replaces) and `halfband_ref_shifted` / `_cq15` (the shift by fs / 4 as a separate pass over the real input, then `halfband_decimate`: what `real_to_complex` replaces), appended at the end of both kernel lists (the seed of an entry is its position). They are named `halfband_ref_*`, not `fir_decimate_*`, because `cargo measure fir_decimate` selects every entry named `fir_decimate_...` and would run the workloads with the FIR family. `halfband_decimate_real` and `halfband_decimate_real_q15` have no own-kernel comparison (there is no real `fir_decimate`) | the maintainer decision asks for the work-saving evidence as information; a separate workload entry is the simplest way to count it with the machinery of the table (differential count, accuracy, misaligned variants), and the names keep the selection rule of `cargo measure` intact |
| 2026-10-31 | M23 | D94 (implementer decision): the fallback of the `f32`/`C32` halfband kernels adds the centre tap after the even-index taps (the even indices ascending, then the centre; `single` and the groups alike, so the result stays independent of the chunking), not between the even indices `m - 1` and `m + 1` as D73 had it; `real_to_complex` is not affected (its real part has no centre tap, its imaginary part is the centre tap alone). The `CQ15`/`Q15` sums are exact integers and do not depend on the order | the accuracy rule (at most 1 dB below the reference) failed for the fallback entries against liquid-dsp's portable code: 140.1 dB against 142.1 dB for `halfband_decimate_fallback` and 140.1 against 142.0 dB for `halfband_decimate_real_fallback` (liquid adds the delay branch, i.e. the centre tap, to the finished filter branch). A numpy model of the 47 taps (20000 samples, `f32` sums) gives 140.6 dB with the centre in place, 142.6 dB with the centre last, 139.0 dB with the centre first: the large term should not be in the middle of the chain. After the change the count input gives 142.1 / 142.5 dB against 142.1 / 142.0 dB (rule met); the golden guards (141.9 / 142.1 dB) and the contract (110 dB) are unaffected. The maintainer decision 3 says that the fallback stays in ascending tap order without FMA; it was about the pre-add, which the fallback does not use, and only the position of one tap changes, but this is a departure from the letter of D73 and is stated here for confirmation. The tests that compared the `f32` result with `fir_decimate` by 2 bit for bit now compare with the two dense filters added in `f32` (the even taps alone, then the centre tap alone), which is the same sum |
| 2026-10-31 | M23 | D95 (implementer decision): the group engine of the halfband fallback (supersedes the group function of D73; the sum rules are unchanged) copies the samples at the even offsets of the window of a group once into a stack array (`real_to_complex`: with the sign of the shift applied then, once per sample instead of once per tap and sample), so that the products of a tap are with consecutive samples and the compiler vectorizes them (no `step_by`, no per-element sign selection); the array is allocated once per call and filled in place; `Q15`/`CQ15` sums run in `i32` when the sum of the absolute taps is at most 65535 raw (the bound of D84: `32768 * 65535 + 2^14 < 2^31`, so every partial sum and the rounding constant are exact and the result is the `i64` sum of `Acc64`), else in `i64` as before; filters of more than 511 taps are computed one output at a time (the stack array holds `m + 8` samples) | the fallbacks missed their limits at most levels (instructions per sample, ours / reference, before the change): `real_to_complex_fallback` 5.4x liquid-dsp at `sse2`, 6.3x at `avx2`, 4.85x on `neon`; `halfband_decimate_real_fallback` 1.5x to 2.5x; `halfband_decimate_cq15_fallback` 1.2x to 2.3x CMSIS-DSP; `halfband_decimate_real_q15_fallback` 3.1x to 3.6x. Counts before -> after (`neon`): 139.37 -> 48.61 (`halfband_decimate_fallback`), 186.65 -> 52.24 (`_cq15_fallback`), 137.45 -> 40.70 (`_real_fallback`), 140.51 -> 42.16 (`_real_q15_fallback`), 457.95 -> 65.23 (`real_to_complex_fallback`), 512.52 -> 63.61 (`real_to_complex_cq15_fallback`); `avx2`: 212.00 -> 45.14, 307.74 -> 57.31, 198.23 -> 41.87, 273.75 -> 51.16, 534.54 -> 64.02, 629.84 -> 68.31. Steps on `neon` for `_real_q15_fallback`: 140.51 (before) -> 87.27 (even copy, windows) -> 60.86 (`i32` sums) -> 44.68 (array allocated once per call, not per group; the zeroing of 526 bytes per group was a `memset` call) -> 42.16 (final). Variants not taken: summing `Q15` in `i64` with the copy (87.27 on `neon`: 2.4x the products of the `i32` form); a group of 8 on wasm32 (see D96). `tests/kernels_m23.rs` checks the edge of the narrow accumulator (absolute tap sums 30000, 65534 to 65537 and 90000 at 11 to 63 taps with extreme samples, against `fir_decimate`), the filters above the group limit (511 to 1023 taps) and `real_to_complex` against the explicit shift for both |
| 2026-10-31 | M23 | D96 (implementer decision): on wasm32 the group has 16 outputs (8 elsewhere) and the `Q15`/`CQ15` sums of the fallback use `core::arch::wasm32` value intrinsics (`i32x4_extmul_low/high_i16x8`, `i32x4_add`, the vector loaded with `bytemuck::cast` of `[i16; 8]`) when `simd128` is on; `sdrk-core` enables the `wasm_simd` feature of `bytemuck` on wasm32 for that (no `unsafe`) | at `simd128` the compiler left the scalar `i32` loop of D95 alone (no `i32x4` instruction in the object code of `sdrk-core`), and the wasmtime fuel counts every operation including `local.get`, so the per-tap work (splat, bounds checks) weighs as much as the products. `halfband_decimate_real_q15_fallback` at `simd128`: 340.44 (before D95) -> 132.52 (D95, scalar) -> 132.58 (intrinsics, group 8: the lane loads of `i16x8(..)` first, then `v128.load` with the same result) -> 100.19 (intrinsics, group 16) against 94.37 for the CMSIS-DSP composition: 1.06x, limit 1.10x. `_cq15_fallback`: 435.68 -> 166.87 -> 134.25 (0.71x); the other fallbacks at `simd128` also fall (`halfband_decimate_fallback` 341.68 -> 122.90, `real_to_complex_fallback` 326.09 -> 103.61). The tests run under wasmtime with `simd128` (`sdrk-core`, `sdrk-backend-simd`) |
| 2026-10-31 | M23 | D97 (implementer decision, SIMD kernels; changes D83): the complex samples stay interleaved in the scratch (the sample `p` places on is `C p` values on, `C` = 1 or 2) and the results go to the output as they are, so there are no planes and no re-interleaving; a sample is copied as one move of a constant width (`split_first_chunk::<W>`) from the whole pairs of input samples (`chunks_exact(2 C)`), the last unpaired sample separately; the `f32` pair loop takes eight vectors of outputs per pass (then four, then one) with the coefficient broadcast once per pair, the tap loop runs over `0 ..= m` so that the compiler sees both slices inside the window (no bounds checks in the loop), and the `Q15` loop runs over the taps from the last for the same reason; the sums of `f32` are within the contract and the symmetric pre-add with FMA stays (D71, D83) | the first version (planar scratch, `step_by` and closure gathers, iterator pairs) cost 136.05 / 62.68 / 75.45 instructions per output sample at `sse2` and 102.14 / 47.48 / 57.73 at `avx2` for `halfband_decimate` / `_real` / `real_to_complex`, of which about 20 were the element-wise gather (25 instructions per element) and the pair loop had 13 instructions of iterator overhead per vector. Steps at `avx2` for `halfband_decimate`: 102.14 -> 28.58 (gathers without `step_by`, slices cut once per pair) -> 19.94 (interleaved, no planes) -> 18.66 (eight vectors) -> 14.82 (constant-width copies) -> 12.77 (scratch size classes, D98); `sse2` 136.05 -> 47.01 -> 37.95 -> 33.30 -> 29.19 -> 28.20. Final counts per output sample: `halfband_decimate` 28.20 / 28.57 / 12.77 / 7.71 / 20.56 / 92.52 (`sse2`, `sse4.2`, `avx2`, `avx512`, `neon`, `simd128`), `halfband_decimate_real` 13.53 / 13.53 / 6.21 / 4.68 / 10.72 / 42.94, `real_to_complex` 21.63 / 21.37 / 10.82 / 7.57 / 16.47 / 52.87. Work-saving evidence against the dense `fir_decimate` by 2 (information, target 0.60): 0.59 / 0.60 / 0.51 / 0.18 at `sse2` / `sse4.2` / `avx2` / `avx512`, 0.70 on `neon` and 0.91 on `simd128`: the target is met on x86-64 and missed on `neon` and `simd128`, where the dense kernel is a tight loop of `fmla`/`f32x4.mul` and the halfband kernel pays for the pair add and the copy; not a gate |
| 2026-10-31 | M23 | D98 (implementer decision, SIMD kernels): the stack scratch and the prepared taps come in two sizes, chosen by the length of the filter (up to 127 taps: even stream for `m <= 63`; up to 1023 taps as before), the padding of 16 values after a block is dropped (reads never go past the next multiple of 16 values inside the block), and the prepared taps are a slice of an array of the class | the zeroing of 5.9 KiB per call (libc `memset`, `rep stosb` above 2 KiB) was a visible part of the cycles of a call of a few hundred samples: with the scratch of the small class the simulated cycles per output sample at `avx2` (`znver3` / `alderlake`, whole-loop) fall for `halfband_decimate_cq15` from 79.89 / 12.96 to 7.54 / 7.18 (`MAX_N` = 127 as an experiment, then the size classes: 7.60 / 7.14), for `halfband_decimate` C32 from 148.70 / 14.70 to 60.40 / 7.50 (the f32 scratch of the small class is 2040 bytes: below the threshold; after the padding was dropped 3.78 / 2.99) and for `halfband_decimate_real_q15` from 76.89 / 9.69 to 4.41 / 3.87. `znver3` rates `rep stosb` at about 50 cycles (see D62), which inflated the first figures; the real saving is the `alderlake` one. Tests: the lengths 127, 131, 255, 515, 1023 and 1027 are in `tests/halfband.rs` (class limits and the fallback above 1023) and pass at `sse2`, `sse4.2`, `avx2` (fixed and detected), `avx512` (SDE), `neon` (QEMU) and `simd128` (wasmtime) |

The kernels themselves are measured at `avx2-runtime` (1.00 to 1.01 of `avx2`) |
| 2026-10-31 | M23 | D100 (implementer decision, `Q15`/`CQ15` SIMD; keeps D85): the `Q15` and `CQ15` halfband kernels stay generic `i32` loops that the compiler vectorizes (`vpmaddwd` appears at `avx2` by itself: the loop is `vpmovzxwd` + `vpmaddwd` + `vpaddd`), no `pmaddwd`/`smlal` path was written | the entries are `info` with an efficiency verdict (no library). `cargo measure` at `avx2` (`znver3` / `alderlake`, whole-loop): `halfband_decimate_cq15` 7.60 / 7.14 cycles per output sample at efficiency 0.79 / 0.95, `halfband_decimate_real_q15` 4.41 / 3.85 at 0.70 / 0.94, `real_to_complex_cq15` 4.74 / 4.87 at 0.80 / 0.91 (the bottleneck is the throughput of the ports, 0.95 of the bound on `alderlake`): there is nothing left for a hand-written path to win at `avx2`. Against our fallback (instructions per output sample, SIMD / fallback): `halfband_decimate_cq15` 0.34 / 0.61 / 0.61 / 0.57 / 0.69 / 0.90 and `halfband_decimate_real_q15` 0.25 / 0.31 / 0.27 / 0.21 / 0.34 / 0.55 at `sse2` / `sse4.2` / `avx2` / `avx512` / `neon` / `simd128`; the weakest are `neon` and `simd128` (the fallback of D95/D96 is itself a vectorized `i32` loop there), where a `smlal` / `i32x4.dot_i16x8` path is the next step if the efficiency verdict of a later round asks for it |

The table has one judged comparison per entry, so the second gate is an entry of its own, `real_to_complex_airspy` (`Family::HilbertAirspy`, appended to both kernel lists, the seed of `real_to_complex`; measured at `sse2` of `x86_64` only, `release`, no entry at the other levels). The results carry `target` and `meets_target` on the `ours:<kernel>` record of `informational`, the console prints `target 0.60: met` or `missed`; nothing fails. `halfband_decimate` and `halfband_decimate_cq15` have 0.60 (the work saved against `fir_decimate` by 2 with the expanded taps), `real_to_complex` and `real_to_complex_cq15` 1.10 (the original default limit of their comparison against the shifted `halfband_decimate`). Measured: the C32 target is met at `sse2` (0.59), `sse4.2` (0.60), `avx2` (0.51), `avx512` (0.18) and missed at `neon` (0.70) and `simd128` (0.91) (I23-3); the others are in the results. `Reference::OwnKernel` stays a tuple variant `OwnKernel(&str)` (the original section writes a struct variant `{ kernel }`; the behaviour is the same) and the workloads are `halfband_ref_*` (D93) | the target becomes machine-checkable (a regression at `sse2` or `sse4.2`, 0.59 and 0.60 against 0.60, shows in the results diff) without making a work-saving figure a gate, which the maintainer decision of 2026-10-31 forbids; tuple against struct variant is a naming matter inside `xtask` |
| 2026-10-31 | M23 | D103 (implementer decision): two golden cases for the saturation edge of the `Q15` halfband kernels, `halfband_decimate/n23_cq15_corners_2048` (seed 452) and `halfband_decimate_real/n23_q15_corners_2048` (seed 453), bit-exact against the independent NumPy integer model (`int64` convolution, round half up, saturation). The input holds windows aligned with the signs of the taps (the sum of the magnitudes is above 1, so the sums reach +32767 and -32768 after the saturation; the case asserts that both limits occur), runs of -32768 and +32767, and full-scale alternations; the second component of the complex case is partly the saturating negation of the first. | 2026-11-01 | M24 | D144: `stereo_pll` `f32` fallback loop: (a) `interp_f32_pair` computes the cosine and the sine of the phase with one fraction (the low 20 bits of `theta - pi/2` equal those of `theta`, the table index is `(idx + 3072) mod 4096`), (b) the integrator clamp is written as two selects that compile to `minss`/`maxss` instead of a compare and blend, (c) one pass over the pilot decides whether any sample is non-finite, and the loop runs as a const-generic variant without the per-sample `is_finite` test when none is (the variant with the test is the former loop), (d) a const-generic mode for `set_enabled`: with the difference channel off the loop does not test the lock decision for the output; it saves 5 instructions per sample at `avx2` (79.4 -> 74.4) and 1 to 4 on the other targets, but makes the simulated cycles of `sse2`, `sse4.2` and `avx2` 2 to 10 percent worse (`avx2`: 63.3 -> 69.4 on the reference model, 81.4 -> 86.5 on the counter-model; the order of the operands of `stereo && enabled` alone moves `sse2` between 88.9 and 92.9 cycles with one instruction more or less, so the loop keeps the order that measured best), so only targets other than x86-64 below AVX-512 use it (`HOIST_ENABLED`; D68: shared code is optimised by the cycles of every level). Bits are unchanged (golden cases, `Reference` comparison and the 20-plan invariance pass). Instructions per sample, delivered state -> now (liquid-dsp 67.94, neon 59.92, simd128 143.86): `sse2` 102.03 -> 90.16 (1.33), `sse4.2` 98.03 -> 88.16 (1.30), `avx2` 88.03 -> 78.38 (1.15), `avx512` 81.03 -> 73.38 (1.08), `neon` 74.03 -> 68.78 (1.15), `simd128` 196.05 -> 190.06 (1.32). The limit 1.10 is met at `avx512` (the waiver of that level is removed) and still missed at the other five levels; not relaxed, open item I24-1 updated. Rejected: the lock decision as a loop mode (one loop for stereo, one for mono, left after the sample that changes the decision, so that the hysteresis is one comparison): `avx2` 78.4 against 74.4 for the hoisted variant, and it needs `off < on` as a validated invariant. Not done: a split of the loop into a serial phase pass and a vector pass (the stores and reloads cost more than they save, the lock detector and the outputs need `s` and `s2` of the same phase), and an `f32` integer form of the phase step (`floor(f 2^32 + 0.5)` through the magic number in `f32`, estimated 4 to 5 instructions fewer, valid for `abs(f) < 2^-10` only, not enough to reach 1.10 at `sse2`, `sse4.2` and `avx2`) | the fallback is the contract (D116, D117), so only the form may change; the changes remove 6 to 15 instructions per sample at every level with no bit change and no regression of the simulated cycles at another target (the hoist of the flag is limited to the targets where it does not cost cycles). The remaining gap is the work that liquid does not do (lock detector, finite test, integrator limit, two denormal flushes, the `f64` phase step required bit for bit); it needs a maintainer decision (I24-1) |

| 2026-11-01 | issue #20 | D166 (implementer decision): SIMD `power_db` (`f32`, `Q15`) picks its loop form per level by the simulated cycles of that level on both of its models, with the instruction gate as a hard limit for the `release` entries; it stays bit-exact with the fallback (`libm::log10f` step by step). Forms: blocks of 4 vectors in three passes for `f32` at `sse2` and `avx2` and for `Q15` at `sse2`, `sse4.2` and `avx2`; a two-stage software pipeline in registers for `f32` at `sse4.2` and for both types at `avx512` and `neon`; one pass on `simd128` (instructions unchanged for `f32`, fewer for `Q15`). On every level the reduction takes `k` as one arithmetic shift and the divisor as `m + 1`, and `+inf` is carried in `k` (proven equal, tested exhaustively over every reduced mantissa). Simulated cycles per sample (whole-loop) `f32` against VOLK `log2` + `s32f_multiply` + `s32f_add`, before / after (VOLK): `sse4.2` 8.72 / 9.83 -> 6.83 / 7.79 (4.62 / 4.98, `skylake` / `znver2`), `sse2` 8.73 / 10.08 -> 5.43 / 5.30 (generic VOLK 59.69 / 54.12), `avx2` 2.17 / 1.90 -> 1.85 / 1.88 (1.18 / 1.52, `znver3` / `alderlake`), `avx512` 1.54 / 1.58 -> 1.50 / 1.56 (1.01 / 0.97, `znver4` / `sapphirerapids`), `neon` 15.91 / 6.84 -> 14.36 / 6.61 (8.24 / 5.45); `Q15` up to 48 % fewer (`sse4.2` `znver2` 9.76 -> 5.08). `sse4.2` instructions per sample 14.77 -> 14.52 (1.07 of VOLK). Rejected: a three-stage pipeline at `sse4.2` (6.03 / 5.85 cycles, but 1.106 of VOLK's instructions, above the release limit), the NEON `frecpe`/Newton division (slower on both models). Integration: `power_db` `Q15` at `avx2-runtime` counts 6.69 against 6.49 instructions per sample of the fixed `avx2` build (1.03, limit 1.02; 6.66 / 6.65 before), the same hot loop but for register allocation inside the `vectorize_avx2` wrapper (213 against 207 instructions per block of 32 samples); no gross outlier, so the entry is information (D68: the instruction counts only catch gross outliers) | the bit-exact contract rules out VOLK's polynomial, so scheduling and exact algebra are the only levers; the remaining gap to VOLK at `sse4.2`, `avx512` (`sapphirerapids` port bound 1.256 against VOLK's 0.967 total) and `cortex-a72` (9.65 against 8.24) is the cost of the `log10f` division and its exact steps |

| 2026-11-01 | issue #20 | D170 (implementer decision, D40 style per-target scalar form): the `C32` FFT fallback runs three radix-2 stages per pass over groups of 8 samples off x86 (AArch64, wasm32, the embedded targets; twiddles read by a running index from 7 windows that share bounds checks; one pair first for `log2 N mod 3 = 2`, one single stage last for `mod 1`); x86 keeps the pairs (`TRIPLE_STAGES`, a `cfg` on `target_arch`; triples were slower there: `avx2` `fft_1024` 20.20 / 20.51 -> 23.58 / 27.10). The `C32` bit reversal visits only even `i < N/2` with `rev(i+1) = rev(i) + N/2` and `rev(i+N/2+1) = rev(i) + N/2 + 1`, three swaps per iteration (`rbit` off x86, a carried reversed counter on x86 and wasm32). Same butterflies, operands and permutation, so the same bits. Simulated cycles per sample, AArch64 build, `cortex-a72` / `cortex-a76` before -> after (CMSIS-DSP 1.18.0): `fft_64` 28.31 / 26.85 -> 21.69 / 20.29 (22.44 / 24.31), `fft_1024` 41.36 / 43.14 -> 33.24 / 34.77 (38.54 / 39.16), `fft_4096` 48.54 / 52.02 -> 37.38 / 39.15 (39.41 / 43.25), `rfft_1024` 22.47 / 23.21 -> 18.29 / 17.01 (18.65 / 18.61). Scalar only (`-C no-vectorize-loops -C no-vectorize-slp`, not committed): `fft_64` 25.63 / 28.73 -> 21.15 / 23.29, `fft_1024` 38.88 / 45.14 -> 33.20 / 38.08, `fft_4096` 46.07 / 54.03 -> 37.59 / 42.46, `rfft_1024` 29.39 / 31.51 -> 21.21 / 24.64, so the gain is the scalar code (Cortex-M has no NEON). x86 7 to 13 % fewer cycles on every level from the bit reversal alone. Cortex-M4 `fft` 1024: 133.79 -> 107.11 instructions per sample; `simd128` 177.17 / 290.17 / 349.97 / 156.40 -> 121.02 / 220.35 / 244.92 / 105.27 | CMSIS-DSP's radix-8 passes touch memory once per three stages and its table-driven bit reversal has no per-index test: these were the gaps of 1.20 to 1.26 on `cortex-a72`; 32 floating-point registers hold eight samples and seven twiddles off x86, 16 xmm registers do not |
| 2026-11-01 | issue #20 | D171 (implementer decision): the `CQ15` fixed-scaling FFT fallback checks every sample for `|x|^2 <= 32700^2` and every twiddle the plan uses for `|w|^2 <= (2^15 + 1)^2` before the stages (the twiddles of `new()` reach 32768.7). Within these bounds no clamp can act in any of up to 16 stages (one stage raises the largest modulus to at most `R (1 + 2^-16) + 1.07`, so it stays below 32726, and every `a ± t` below 65533), so the stages run in `i32` without clamps, in pairs (radix-2^2 groups of 4 samples with 3 twiddles; twiddle-outer loop order while there are fewer twiddles than blocks), with the products accumulated onto the rounding base (`s = b + w c` and `(2b - 1) - s`). On 64-bit targets and wasm32 only: the embedded 32-bit targets keep the clamped code (Cortex-M4 `ssat` with shift; the fast path counted 347 against 328 instructions per sample there). Simulated cycles per sample, `fft_cq15_1024_default_fallback` before -> after (CMSIS-DSP `arm_cfft_q15`): `neon` 112.44 / 92.76 -> 50.88 / 51.01 (64.42 / 52.33), `avx2` 38.67 / 45.88 -> 28.69 / 31.21 (38.91 / 39.38), `sse2` 62.64 / 79.41 -> 50.23 / 54.76 (44.75 / 56.09), `sse4.2` 76.03 / 66.12 -> 45.87 / 46.88 (44.80 / 56.09), `avx512` 33.75 / 43.62 -> 28.62 / 32.22 (38.80 / 40.50); instructions per sample `neon` 242.09 -> 135.19 (140.66), `sse2` 289.2 -> 210.45 (217.26), `simd128` 454.32 -> 405.94 (378.19). Scalar only on AArch64 (`-C no-vectorize-loops -C no-vectorize-slp`): 254.79 -> 153.28 instructions, 115.52 / 94.23 -> 55.22 / 57.42 cycles. The entry is `release` at `sse2`, `neon` and `simd128` (was `info` with a waiver). Rejected: single stages after the first pair on x86 (`avx2` 36.84 / 36.04 against 29.06 / 30.60) | the clamps were the serial cost of the stages; the bound makes them provably inactive, so they can go without a change of the contract |

| 2026-11-01 | M25 | D177: the release step of the `Q15` envelope, `env = round_shift(env * r_q31, 31)`, is computed with an `i128` product (the specification names `i128` only for the pre-emphasis and the division) | the envelope is Q30 and reaches `2^39` with 36 dB of pre-emphasis gain (noise of sigma 0.2 in `preemph_q15_8192`; `v` up to about 600 for 1000 us at 240 kHz); the `i64` product with the Q31 factor overflows above `env = 4.0` and panics with overflow checks; where an `i64` product would not overflow the bits are the same, and the rustdoc and the golden model (Python integers) state it |

No clamp of `x` was added; the input range is documented in `docs/kernels.md` and open item I25-1 asks for a maintainer decision | a clamp of `x` would change the specified arithmetic, and the right limit (full scale, 20 times, `f32::MAX / b0`) is a policy choice; the contract of a transmitter chain is audio within a few times full scale, the property tests use 20 times, and `extremes_m25` keeps `f32::MAX` out of the strict checks |
| 2026-11-01 | M25 | D179: the `Q15` output clamp to plus or minus `T_q15` with `T_q15 = min(floor(T * 32768 + 0.5), 32767)` as specified means that `-32768` is never an output, also with the limiter idle at limit 1.0 and with `[1, 0, 0]`: the input `-32768` comes out as `-32767`. The test 'a tone below the limit passes unchanged' excludes exactly that value, and a test asserts the exception | the clamp is symmetric by design (a limiter must not output a larger magnitude than its positive limit), one LSB at the negative corner is below the quantization of any audio path, and an asymmetric clamp would change the specified arithmetic |

| 2026-11-01 | M25 | D189: the loop tests are built from small helpers in `tests/system/src/lib.rs` (`flow_value`, `spec_from_value`, `set_precision`, `remove_node`, `VecAudio`, `transmit_bytes`, `receive_bytes`, `peak_deviation`, `band_limited_noise`). The transmitter runs in pull mode, its bytes are pushed into the receiver in pieces of 8192 bytes (no channel, no loopback). The Q15 variants set `settings.precision` and `inputs.audio.type` to `q15`, with a pull source that converts with saturation. The peak meter is always an `f32` flow (`ddc` 2.4 MS/s to 240 kS/s with 100 kHz cutoff and 40 kHz transition for 75 kHz deviation, 25 kHz and 15 kHz for 5 kHz deviation; `fm_demod` with `deemphasis_us` 0 and `audio_decimation` 1; the first 2000 output samples are skipped), because the Q15 discriminator saturates at 1.0 and could not show 1.02. TX-FLAT measures 0.15 s of each 0.3 s tone, starting 0.1 s after the tone begins. The noise is `Pcg32::new(450, 1)` through two RBJ low-pass sections for a fourth-order Butterworth at 15 kHz (`f64`), scaled to sigma 3 after the filter (`sigma` means the standard deviation of the filtered signal); it is generated in the test and is not a golden vector. The NFM tests measure the SINAD over the second half of the 0.5 s tone (the squelch opens within 1 ms and the filters settle within 0.1 s) | the milestone leaves these choices open; each is the simplest reading that keeps the specified thresholds, and the measurement chain follows the text of section 6 (ddc to 240 kHz plus `quad_demod` with the stated gain) |

| 2026-11-01 | M25 | D200: the `f32` variant of `preemph_limit` clamps every finite input to `[-1024, 1024]` (60 dB above full scale) after replacing NaN and infinities by 0, and the Q15 negative control of TX-PEAK is the structural bound of 0.9 to 1.1 (measured 1.043; with `shape` 0.983). This closes I25-1 and I25-2 and amends D178 and D191. Simulated with the float32 model of the golden generator before the change: a single finite input broke the state (NaN until `reset`) from 1.03e38 (tau 50 us, 48 kHz) down to 6.9e35 (tau 1 ms, 384 kHz), so the clamp has a margin of at least 6.7e32 to the first failure; with the clamp the largest pre-emphasized value for alternating inputs of +-1024 over tau 50 us to 1 ms and 40 to 384 kHz is 1.03e6 (the filter pole is `a1` = 0.1338 for every design, so the sum stays finite) and no output or state became non-finite; 144 runs with inputs up to +-1024 (noise, peaks, all coefficient sets, limits 0.1 to 1.0) gave the same bits with and without the clamp. The 21 committed golden files of the kernel are unchanged; the new case `clamp_f32_4096` (seed 0, fixed positions) pins the clamp; `a_huge_finite_input_is_clamped`, the corners test and `extremes_m25` cover it. An instability from coefficients with `abs(a1) >= 1` is not an input problem and is not changed. | A corrupted sample (an uninitialized buffer, a bit error, an upstream gain block gone wrong) must not leave a transmitter on NaN until the stream is reset, and the clamp costs one compare on the common path and changes no valid input. The Q15 control cannot exceed 1.5 by construction (its input saturates at full scale), so the specification states what it can show: that the node, not the input, holds the peak. |

Implementer decisions for `cargo xtask release-check`: (1) the inheritance check covers the members of the root workspace; the crates of `targets/*` are workspaces of their own and get the version 0.1.0 written out (with their lock files), `crates/pipeline/sdrk-pipeline/fuzz` (0.0.0, unpublished) and `benches` (0.0.0, `publish = false`) keep theirs; (2) "names the old version" means `version = "0.0.1"`, `"version": "0.0.1"`, `version 0.0.1` or `version stays 0.0.1` in a tracked text file, skipping lock files and the fixtures of `xtask/tests/` and of the check itself besides the records of §2; (3) an `issue #N` reference is accepted as written (no network access); (4) `--previous <v>` names the old version for later releases (default 0.0.1); (5) the CI job `release_check` of `ci.yml` runs on pull requests whose title starts with `Release` and `result` accepts it as skipped otherwise; (6) the WebAssembly check of Tests 3 is `cargo xtask wasm-pkg` (the milestone wrote `cargo xtask wasm`) | the M25.1 text predates the targets as separate workspaces and the test fixtures; the check must pass on a correct tree |

| 2026-11-03 | M28 | D314: supersedes the cause given in D312 and the advice of D313. The 441-sample steps of the fill in the `resample_audio` test do not come from the resampler: the tunable `resample` consumes every push completely and works sample by sample (measured over 600 s: at most 0 input samples pushed but not consumed by `to_cd`, at most 0 output samples not yet drained into the queue, only its fractional read position of under 2 output samples stays inside). They come from the phase between the input clock (blocks of 480 every 480/(48000 (1 + 50e-6)) s) and the read clock (blocks of 441 every 10 ms): read only after each read, the queue is a sawtooth that jumps by one block each time the two clocks slip by one period (the fill is exactly 4410 for the first 180 s, the first jump comes at about 190 s). The fix is a calling contract, not code: `PipelineDrift::update` is called immediately before every `push` and immediately before every read, so each call sees the level that held since the previous call and the dt-weighted smoothing of the controller averages the queue over time, which moves continuously with the clock phase. Measured over 600 s with the defaults (tau 30 s, target 4410), ppm in 300 to 600 s: after every read only (before) mean -28.31, min -496.47, max 34.84 (whole run min -499.20), fill 4288 to 4851; after every push and after every read mean -0.27, min -500.00, max 237.49, fill 3781 to 4848; before every push and before every read (chosen) mean -50.09, min -51.74, max -49.72, and from 180 s on within -51.74 to -49.72, queue 3969 to 4410. `docs/pipeline.md`, the rustdoc of `PipelineDrift`, `examples/README.md` and the YAML comment of `resample_audio` state the contract; the advice of D313 (a target of several blocks and a longer `smoothing_s`) is removed, and open item I28-2 is closed | the maintainer chose the usage fix: it needs no API change and leaves the control law of `DriftController` exactly as specified, and it is the only one of the three call patterns that settles; a continuous fill estimate from the input pending inside the pipeline (the first decision) adds at most about 2 samples here and cannot remove the steps, because the pipeline never sees the phase of the input clock |

| 2026-11-03 | M30 | D321: a SIMD entry must not be slower than its own fallback in simulated cycles per sample, on the reference model **and** on the counter-model of its level, and this is a gate: `cargo xtask check cycles` (part of `cargo xtask check`, so CI) reads the committed cycle tables of `docs/performance.md` and fails for every entry `<k>` with a row `<k>_fallback` on the same level whose column "ours" (two decimals, as published) is above that of the fallback on either model. A level delegated to the fallback (comparison `sdrk fallback`, both ratios 1.00) passes; a violation passes only with a `cycle_waiver` on the SIMD entry and level in `benches/limits.json` that names an open item of `docs/open-items.md`, and a `cycle_waiver` whose gap is closed (or whose pair does not exist) fails the check too. `cargo measure` prints the same verdict (`SIMD vs fallback ...`) for every pair it simulates. This changes D32, which treated "SIMD branch against our own fallback" as information only: it stays no judged comparison in instructions, but in cycles it is now a floor. It restates the speed target: cycles per sample (`llvm-mca`, D33, D37, D68) are what is optimised and judged; instructions per sample are the speed measure only where no core model exists (`simd128`, the in-order cores) and elsewhere only catch gross outliers | the maintainer's position: a hand-written SIMD path has to be at least as fast as the scalar fallback, which LLVM may auto-vectorise, ideally faster; under D32 thirteen M30 paths were slower than their fallbacks on one or both models (up to 1.19) without anyone noticing, because the cycles were reported but nothing compared these two rows. The tables are the source because CI runs no `llvm-mca` (it stays fast) and every merged row already went through `benches/cycle_tables.py`; the two decimals are what the tables publish, so the rule is reproducible from the document alone |

Every prepared tap is split once per call into `256 hi + lo`, `lo` its low byte as a signed value and `hi = (t - lo) / 256`, both at most 128 in magnitude: a pair of products of two samples with `hi` or with `lo` is at most `2^23` in magnitude, so a window of at most 96 vectors of taps (the storage of the prepared branches) sums exactly in 32-bit lanes (below `2^30`), and the output is `256 sum hi + sum lo` in `i64` with the rounding and saturation of `Acc64::finish`. `CQ15` reorders every vector of samples to `re0 re1 im0 im1 re2 re3 im2 im3` with `pshufb` (as `dot` `CQ15`) and the prepared taps in the same order once per call. The outputs of one branch (for `fir` and `fir_decimate` all outputs of the call) have windows `M` samples apart (`M = 1` for `fir` and `fir_interpolate`), so four of them share each pair of tap vectors, eight independent chains of `paddd`; the outputs whose windows reach into the history or past the end of the input go one at a time, each vector of samples read in place or assembled where it straddles the history and the input. Exact integer sums in another order, so the same bits. SSE2 (fallback by `runs_fallback`), AVX2, AVX-512, NEON and SIMD128 keep their paths, their source unchanged: the SSE4.2 path prepares its taps with copies of the two methods of `Q15Engine` (`prepare_taps`, `prepare_branch_taps`, x86 only), because a first version in which the methods called these functions changed the code LLVM generated for the old engine (`fir_cq15` 227.65 to 234.65 instructions per sample at `neon`, 841.39 to 848.17 at `simd128`, `resample_cq15` 296.92 to 299.95 at `neon`). The backend tests `cq15_filters_are_bit_exact_in_blocks_at_full_scale` and `resample_real_q15_is_bit_exact_in_blocks_at_full_scale` compare all six with the fallback over calls of every size with little output room, taps and samples at full scale (all `-32768`, all `32767`, alternating) and the longest filters that fit (256 taps of `fir`, 768 lanes of prepared branches) | simulated cycles per sample, SIMD / fallback, `skylake` / `znver2`, before and after: `fir_cq15` 176.75 / 142.31 and 119.09 / 187.71, after 47.80 / 142.31 and 49.03 / 187.71; `fir_decimate_cq15` 39.18 / 30.41 and 27.75 / 45.20, after 8.90 / 30.41 and 9.34 / 45.20; `fir_interpolate_cq15` 249.66 / 137.49 and 144.54 / 207.97, after 51.51 / 137.49 and 53.45 / 207.97; `fir_interpolate_real_q15` 136.89 / 74.97 and 88.20 / 112.89, after 26.86 / 74.97 and 32.49 / 112.89; `resample_cq15` 249.52 / 168.56 and 143.48 / 240.62, after 50.01 / 168.56 and 51.67 / 240.62; `resample_real_q15` 136.55 / 103.68 and 87.57 / 144.23, after 26.11 / 103.68 and 31.59 / 144.23. Before, every output was its own dot product: per vector of eight samples two widenings to `i32`, two `pmulld` (two micro-operations each on the `skylake` model), four widenings to `i64` and four `paddq`, and a horizontal sum per output; now a vector of taps costs one load, one `pshufb` (`CQ15`), two `pmaddwd` and two `paddd` per output, the tap vectors shared by four outputs, and one horizontal sum of two vectors per output. The other options of the item were not needed: delegating the level would have kept the fallback's 142.31 to 168.56 cycles; splitting the samples instead of the taps (as `dot` does, where the taps are not reused) costs a shift and a mask per vector of samples and output instead of a split per tap and call, and was not simulated. Instructions per sample at `sse4.2`, before / after (the entries stay `info` against the fallback): `fir_cq15` 417.99 / 187.33, `fir_decimate_cq15` 87.46 / 35.72, `fir_interpolate_cq15` 510.19 / 201.70, `fir_interpolate_real_q15` 315.00 / 114.51, `resample_cq15` 506.50 / 196.06, `resample_real_q15` 313.14 / 111.86; `avx2` unchanged, `avx2-runtime` within 0.01 (`biquad_cq15` and `fir_decimate_cq15` 18.06 to 18.07 and 50.14 to 50.15, the test of the level in the entry), `avx512` unchanged in the counts of the six filters, `neon` and `simd128` source-identical (recounted: `fir_cq15` 227.65 and 841.39 as committed) |

| 2026-11-06 | M39.1 | D720 (maintainer decision on I39.1-2: adapt rtl_433, `src/pulse_detect.c` `pulse_detect_package`): the level trackers of `ook_demod` (§4) are decision-directed means instead of the peak trackers: with `t = (hi + lo) / 2` of the levels before a symbol, a symbol above `t` is a 1 and moves `hi` by `d = 1 - exp(-1 / (0.5 track_symbols))` of its distance (the step of §4), any other symbol is a 0 and moves `lo` by `1 - exp(-1 / (8 track_symbols))`. Mapping of rtl_433's rates (1/64 per sample for the high level during a pulse, 1/1024 per sample for the low level while idle): their ratio 16 is kept (`OOK_LOW_RATIO`), the absolute time constant comes from `track_symbols` (16 on-symbols, 256 off-symbols at the default 32), because the boxcar already averages one symbol, so each symbol value is one independent observation, unlike rtl_433's per-sample envelope. Lead-in (rtl_433 waits 1024 idle samples, one low time constant, before it detects a pulse): the first `track_symbols` symbols use the peak trackers of §4, which give the means their start without assuming an idle channel (rtl_433's start value `high = 9 dB above low` would miss the test 4 signal, whose on level is 1.55 times the off level after the boxcar). After more than `track_symbols` equal decisions in a row the other level also moves towards the symbol with the slow step, the analogue of rtl_433's return to idle after an over-long pulse, so the trackers recover from a noise floor that steps above the threshold. rtl_433's hysteresis `t / 8` is not used: in a float64 simulation of test 4 with ideal timing (150 frames) it raised the PER from 0.13 to 0.99 at 50 kHz and from 0.007 to 0.13 at 15 kHz, since `t / 8` exceeds the eye at this SNR. Before `symbol_sync` the boxcar output is multiplied by `1 / max(hi - lo, OOK_MIN_SWING)` (1e-6), fixed at the first sample of each block of `OOK_DEMOD_CHUNK` (256) input samples since construction or reset (pieces never cross a block, so the block stays chunk invariant; 1 before the first symbol), so the swing is about 1 and the loop runs at its nominal gain (I39.1-2 cause (c)); `TED_GAIN_OOK` keeps its meaning: the measured Gardner slope at 8 and 125 samples per symbol is 0.3703 and 0.3626 both for the levels 0 / 1 and for the test 4 levels 1.82 / 2.82 (a common level adds no error). The trackers work on the symbols divided by the gain again; the soft bit is `(v - t)` times the gain (about +-0.5 for a clean signal at any level). Kind cost `5 + round(sps) + 28 / sps` (one multiply per sample more, D712) | the maintainer chose the reference implementation rtl_433; the simulation of 150 frames with ideal timing gave the means the PER of the ideal fixed threshold (0.13 at 50 kHz, 0.007 at 15 kHz) against 0.37 and 0.11 for the peak trackers; measured on test 4 (seed 543): PER 0.268 (134 of 500) with the flow of §4, 0.010 (5 of 500) with a 15 kHz channel filter |

| 2026-11-06 | M39.1 | D722 (maintainer decision on I39.1-1: adapt dump1090 `demodulate2000`, `mode_s.c` `decodeModesMessage` and `icao_filter.c`): after an output frame the search continues behind it, after a failed or rejected candidate at `p + 1`; only DF 0, 4, 5, 11, 16, 17, 18, 20, 21 and 24 are decoded (every other DF value, also 25 to 31, is dropped). The syndrome is the CRC-24 of the frame without its last 24 bits xor those bits (`modesChecksum`). DF 17 and 18 with syndrome 0 are output with `CRC_OK` (14 bytes), DF 11 with syndrome 0 with `CRC_OK` (7 bytes); these frames, and only these (no error correction, so no corrected bits), put their address (bytes 1 to 3) into an ICAO address cache. The address/parity frames, DF 0, 4, 5, 16, 20, 21, 24 and DF 11 with a nonzero syndrome, are output with flag bit 16 only when their syndrome is in the cache, otherwise dropped; the flag is renamed `ADSB_SHORT_FRAME` -> `ADSB_ADDRESS_PARITY` (it now also marks long frames; no alias before 1.0). The cache (heap-free, caller storage, 4096 `u32`): two tables of `ADSB_ICAO_SLOTS` 2048 slots, open addressing with linear probing and dump1090's `icaoHash` (Jenkins one-at-a-time over the three bytes), a used slot holds `address | 1 << 24`; at most `ADSB_ICAO_CAPACITY` 1024 addresses per table (half the slots, so a lookup probes few slots); eviction: no single address is evicted, a full active table takes no new address (as dump1090 when its table is full), and every `ADSB_ICAO_TTL` = 120000000 input samples (60 s at 2 MHz, `MODES_ICAO_FILTER_TTL` 60000 ms) since construction or reset the older table is cleared and becomes the active one, so an address lives 60 to 120 s of input; the flips are applied lazily at the input index of the frame that uses the cache (several due flips at once clear both tables), so the output does not depend on the chunking. DF 11 with a nonzero syndrome is looked up by its syndrome as the brief says (a real DF 11 has the interrogator code there and is then dropped unless it equals a cached address); DF 20 and 21 are matched exactly, without dump1090's disabled fuzzy match | the maintainer's decision; the address/parity frames found in the noise (I39.1-1 cause (b), 7 such packets on test 5) are no longer output, and the three frames they hid (20765, 76761, 80978) decode |
| 2026-11-06 | M39.1 | D723: the out-of-phase retry of dump1090 (`detectOutOfPhase`, `applyPhaseCorrection`) is adopted: a candidate that passed the preamble and level tests and is not output is decoded once more from a copy of its 241 samples (the one before `p` and the frame) after the phase correction, when its preamble shows energy beside the pulses (`m[p+3] > m[p+2] / 3`, `m[p+10] > m[p+9] / 3`, `m[p+6] > m[p+7] / 3` or `m[p-1] > m[p+1] / 3`); the sample before `p` always exists (the window, zeros at the start), so no position is excluded as dump1090 does at the start of its buffer. Measured on test 5: 184 of 185 frames with it, 183 without (it recovers 49145 at 10.17 dB, one bit error), no false frames either way; cold path only | the brief asks to adopt it only if it measurably helps |

With the default transition of the kind (0.1 of the output rate, 25 kHz; the stop band starts at `cutoff + transition`) the 15 kHz filter passes noise up to 40 kHz. Test 4 runs on the shipped flow at the values of the specification with the threshold PER < 1 % unchanged; the 15 kHz comparison test is removed (it is now the test itself) and the noise-free loop at the 10 kHz offset stays | the magnitude is taken before the boxcar, so every bit of noise bandwidth of the channel filter reaches the decision; a transition of 10 kHz costs about 2.5 times the `ddc` taps of the default (the Kaiser length grows with the inverse of the transition) and is the cheapest way to make the decided cutoff effective |

The timing loop keeps the bandwidth 0.01. Measured (test 4, seed 543, 500 frames, test profile): loop bandwidth 0.003 with the midpoint and the default transition PER 0.010 (5 of 500, unchanged) and a burst after silence no longer locks within the 32 preamble bits (21 wrong bits after the sync word at one of 16 start phases; with 0.01 every bit is right from bit 18 on, new test `ook_demod_locks_within_the_preamble_of_the_test_framing`); threshold 0.40 with the default transition PER 0.028 (14 of 500); with the transition of D726: wrong payload bits (outside the preamble and the gaps, 46273 bits) 2 at 0.40, 1 at 0.42, 0 at 0.44 to 0.46, 3 at 0.48 and 0.50, and the final run PER 0 (500 of 500); a check with another seed (777, transition of D726) gives 0 wrong payload bits at every threshold from 0.40 to 0.50 and PER 0 at the midpoint | the timing loop is not the limit: the errors were 1 read as 0 at the midpoint, since the envelope of an on symbol (Rician) spreads about 1.5 times more than that of an off symbol (Rayleigh; measured 0.142 and 0.096 of the swing); the point of equal distance is 0.40, but the off symbols between two on symbols have a longer tail, so 0.45 lies in the middle of the measured optimum; the narrower loop would cost the lock within the preamble |

| 2026-11-06 | M43 | D800 (maintainer decision of 2026-11-06, the reusable complex dot product): a new kernel `dot_complex`, trait `kernels::dot::DotComplex<T>` with `dot_complex(x, w) -> T`, `sum x[i] w[i]` with complex weights, not conjugated, next to `dot` and `dot_real` in `kernels::dot`; only `C32` is implemented (D802); no conjugated variant is offered; bit-exact class, `Reference` follows the f32 order of the contract step by step (the float32-model rule of the feedback kernels, D111), the accuracy against the f64 sum is a unit test | `dot` multiplies by real taps and its `C32` SIMD paths reorder and fuse (tolerance), so it can neither compute the equalizer's `sum w[i] u[i]` nor give the bit-exact class of M43 §1; the name follows `dot_real` (R1.5); the equalizer needs only the plain product, and a conjugated variant (correlation) can be added when a user needs it |
| 2026-11-06 | M43 | D801: `dot_complex` sums in eight partial sums per component (`DOT_COMPLEX_PARTIALS` = 8, one value for every backend and level): element `i` to partial sum `i mod 8`, the product `(x.re w.re - x.im w.im, x.re w.im + x.im w.re)` in that order without FMA, the partial sums from `+0.0`, then `s[k] = acc[k] + acc[k + 4]`, `t[k] = s[k] + s[k + 2]`, `t[0] + t[1]`; the tail needs no rule of its own (its elements go to their partial sums), so the SIMD paths pad the last block with zeros (an exact `+0` product does not change a partial sum, which is never `-0`) | eight complex lanes are one 256-bit vector per component (AVX2; AVX-512 cannot use more lanes without more partial sums) and two 128-bit vectors (SSE2, SSE4.2, NEON, SIMD128); fewer would leave AVX2 lanes empty and lengthen the chains, more would grow the fixed tree and the zero padding on the 11-tap default filter and the register pressure of the scalar fallback (16 of the 32 single-precision registers of a Cortex-M4F) |

GNU Radio's loop does the same per-symbol work without the tracker and `mse`.
